Based on the search results, here's what I found about LLaVA-PruMerge and ICCV 2025:

LLaVA-PruMerge was presented at ICCV 2025. The paper is titled "LLaVA-PruMerge: Adaptive Token Reduction for Efficient Large Multimodal Models" and was authored by Shang, Yuzhang; Cai, Mu; Xu, Bingxin; Lee, Yong Jae; and Yan, Yan.

The ICCV 2025 papers available in the Open Access Repository are identical to the accepted versions, confirming that the paper was accepted to the conference. The paper appears in the Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF International Conference on Computer Vision (ICCV) 2025, pages 22857-22867.

The paper proposes PruMerge, a novel adaptive visual token reduction strategy that significantly reduces the number of visual tokens without compromising the performance of Large Multimodal Models (LMMs).

Links: [{"title":"Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training [Quick Review]","url":"https://liner.com/review/sigmoid-loss-for-language-image-pretraining"},{"title":"[Paper Note] Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training, Xiaohua Zhai+, ICCV'23 · Issue #2111 · AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes","url":"https://github.com/AkihikoWatanabe/paper_notes/issues/2111"},{"title":"SigLIP: Sigmoid Loss for Language-Image Pre-Training","url":"https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/sigmoid-loss-for-language-image-pre-training-siglip"},{"title":"Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training","url":"https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2023/html/Zhai_Sigmoid_Loss_for_Language_Image_Pre-Training_ICCV_2023_paper.html"},{"title":"[2303.15343] Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.15343"},{"title":"Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training Xiaohua Zhai⋆ Basil Mustafa","url":"https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2023/papers/Zhai_Sigmoid_Loss_for_Language_Image_Pre-Training_ICCV_2023_paper.pdf"},{"title":"Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training","url":"https://www.researchgate.net/publication/377429802_Sigmoid_Loss_for_Language_Image_Pre-Training"},{"title":"Paper page - Sigmoid Loss for Language Image Pre-Training","url":"https://huggingface.co/papers/2303.15343"}]

I found comprehensive information about this paper. Here are the key details:

## Overview

The paper proposes a simple pairwise sigmoid loss for image-text pre-training. Unlike standard contrastive learning with softmax normalization, the sigmoid loss operates solely on image-text pairs and does not require a global view of the pairwise similarities for normalization.

## Key Advantages

The sigmoid loss simultaneously allows further scaling up the batch size, while also performing better at smaller batch sizes. SigLIP fundamentally decouples the loss normalization from batch size and negative sampling. Since each pair contributes independently, SigLIP models achieve stable and high performance across a wide range of batch sizes—from per-example streaming up to million-scale batches.

## Performance Results

With only four TPUv4 chips, we can train a Base CLIP model at 4k batch size and a Large LiT model at 20k batch size, the latter achieves 84.5% ImageNet zero-shot accuracy in two days.

## Publication Details

The paper was written by Xiaohua Zhai, Basil Mustafa, Alexander Kolesnikov, and Lucas Beyer, and published in the Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF International Conference on Computer Vision (ICCV), 2023, pages 11975-11986.

The paper demonstrates that sigmoid loss offers a more efficient approach to language-image pre-training compared to traditional softmax-based contrastive learning methods.

Based on the search results, I found comprehensive information about the TokenLearner paper:

## Summary

TokenLearner introduces a novel visual representation learning approach that relies on a handful of adaptively learned tokens, applicable to both image and video understanding tasks. Instead of relying on hand-designed splitting strategies to obtain space-time tokens from videos, the approach learns to mine important tokens in video frames, resulting in efficiently and effectively finding a few important visual tokens and enabling modeling of pairwise interactions between such tokens over a longer temporal horizon.

## Key Technical Details

TokenLearner is a learnable module to be placed within Transformer architectures for images and videos that significantly reduces the number of tokens for all subsequent layers, thereby reducing the overall computation. The TokenLearner module learns to spatially attend over a subset of tensor pixels from intermediate spatial representations and generates a set of token vectors adaptive to the input.

## Publication Details

The paper was published as part of Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 34 (NeurIPS 2021) by Michael Ryoo, AJ Piergiovanni, Anurag Arnab, Mostafa Dehghani, and Anelia Angelova. The arXiv version (2106.11297) is titled "TokenLearner: What Can 8 Learned Tokens Do for Images and Videos?"

## Results

The experiments demonstrate strong performance on several challenging benchmarks for video recognition tasks, and importantly, due to the tokens being adaptive, the approach accomplishes competitive results at significantly reduced computational cost.

I found the paper you're looking for! The paper "Quantifying Attention Flow in Transformers" by Samira Abnar and Willem Zuidema appears in the Proceedings of the 58th Annual Meeting of the Association for Computational Linguistics, pages 4190–4197.

The paper addresses how in the Transformer model, self-attention combines information from attended embeddings into the representation of the focal embedding in the next layer, causing information from different tokens to become increasingly mixed across layers, which makes attention weights unreliable as explanation probes.

The paper is available at: https://aclanthology.org/2020.acl-main.385/

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's what I found about arXiv 2404.16821:

**Paper Title:** "How Far Are We to GPT-4V? Closing the Gap to Commercial Multimodal Models with Open-Source Suites"

**Overview:** The paper introduces InternVL 1.5, an open-source multimodal large language model (MLLM) to bridge the capability gap between open-source and proprietary commercial models in multimodal understanding.

**Key Improvements:** The paper presents three main improvements:

1. Strong Vision Encoder: exploring a continuous learning strategy for InternViT-6B to boost visual understanding capabilities

2. Dynamic High-Resolution: dividing images into tiles ranging from 1 to 40 of 448×448 pixels according to aspect ratio and resolution, supporting up to 4K resolution input

3. High-Quality Bilingual Dataset: a carefully collected dataset covering common scenes and document images, annotated with English and Chinese question-answer pairs

**Results:** The benchmark results show that InternVL 1.5 achieves performance comparable to leading proprietary models.

The paper was submitted on April 25, 2024, with a revised version on April 29, 2024.

I found the paper successfully! Here are the key details about arXiv 2307.15818:

**Title:** RT-2: Vision-Language-Action Models Transfer Web Knowledge to Robotic Control

**Publication Date:** July 28, 2023

**Core Contribution:** The paper studies how vision-language models trained on Internet-scale data can be incorporated directly into end-to-end robotic control to boost generalization and enable emergent semantic reasoning.

**Main Approach:** The authors propose to co-fine-tune state-of-the-art vision-language models on both robotic trajectory data and Internet-scale vision-language tasks, such as visual question answering. They express the actions as text tokens and incorporate them directly into the training set of the model in the same way as natural language tokens.

**Key Authors:** Lead authors include Anthony Brohan, Noah Brown, Justice Carbajal, Yevgen Chebotar, and 50+ other researchers

The paper introduces RT-2 (Robotics Transformer-2), which represents a significant advancement in combining vision-language models with robotic control by allowing the transfer of knowledge from internet-scale data to robotic tasks.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the arXiv paper 2310.08864 on "Open X-Embodiment: Robotic Learning Datasets and RT-X Models":

## Overview

The paper provides datasets in standardized data formats and models to explore the possibility of creating generalist X-robot policies in the context of robotic manipulation.

## Dataset

The researchers assembled a dataset from 22 different robots collected through a collaboration between 21 institutions, demonstrating 527 skills (160,266 tasks), and a high-capacity model trained on this data called RT-X exhibits positive transfer and improves the capabilities of multiple robots by leveraging experience from other platforms.

The Open X-Embodiment Dataset (OXE) is the largest multi-robot dataset with 1M+ trajectories across 22 robot embodiments, collected from 21 institutions and 34 research labs worldwide.

## Publication

The work was published in the 2024 IEEE International Conference on Robotics and Automation (ICRA), with the project website at robotics-transformer-x.github.io.

I found the paper! Here are the key details about arXiv 2410.16261:

## Mini-InternVL: A Flexible-Transfer Pocket Multimodal Model

Mini-InternVL is a series of MLLMs with parameters ranging from 1B to 4B, which achieves 90% of the performance with only 5% of the parameters.

**Problem Addressed:**
The large model scale and associated high computational costs pose significant challenges for training and deploying MLLMs on consumer-grade GPUs or edge devices, thereby hindering their widespread application.

**Key Features:**

1. **Efficient Architecture:** Mini-InternVL includes a lightweight vision encoder—InternViT-300M, that is robust for various visual domains.

2. **Transfer Learning Framework:** A unified adaptation framework enables the models to transfer and outperform specialized models in downstream tasks, including autonomous driving, medical images, and remote sensing.

3. **Standardized Approach:** The paper introduces a simple but effective paradigm that standardizes model architecture, data format, and training schedule for effective downstream task transfer.

**Performance:**
The multimodal models achieve 90% of the performance using significantly fewer parameters on general multimodal benchmarks, and for specific domain tasks, with minimal computational cost for fine-tuning, they can rival closed-source commercial models.

The paper was submitted on October 21, 2024 (last revised November 7, 2024) and the code is available on GitHub at the OpenGVLab/InternVL repository.

I found the paper successfully! Here's what I found:

A-ViT is a method that adaptively adjusts the inference cost of vision transformers (ViT) for images of different complexity by automatically reducing the number of tokens processed in the network as inference proceeds. 

The method reformulates Adaptive Computation Time (ACT) for this task, extending halting to discard redundant spatial tokens. A-ViT requires no extra parameters or sub-network for halting, as the learning of adaptive halting is based on the original network parameters.

The paper was authored by Hongxu Yin, Arash Vahdat, Jose Alvarez, Arun Mallya, Jan Kautz, and Pavlo Molchanov, and it was accepted as an oral presentation at CVPR 2022.

The paper is available at: https://arxiv.org/abs/2112.07658

I found the arXiv paper 2306.14048. Here's what the search results reveal about this research:

## Overview

The paper introduces a novel approach for implementing the KV cache which significantly reduces its memory footprint, based on the observation that a small portion of tokens contributes most of the value when computing attention scores.

## Key Concept: Heavy Hitters (H₂)

The emergence of Heavy Hitters (H₂) is natural and strongly correlates with the frequent co-occurrence of tokens in the text, and removing them results in significant performance degradation.

## The H₂O Method

The paper proposes Heavy Hitter Oracle (H₂O), a KV cache eviction policy that dynamically retains a balance of recent and H₂ tokens. The KV cache eviction is formulated as a dynamic submodular problem with a theoretical guarantee for the novel eviction algorithm.

## Performance Results

The algorithm's accuracy is validated with OPT, LLaMA, and GPT-NeoX across a wide range of tasks. The implementation of H₂O with 20% heavy hitters improves throughput over three leading inference systems DeepSpeed Zero-Inference, Hugging Face Accelerate, and FlexGen by up to 29×, 29×, and 3× on OPT-6.7B and OPT-30B.

The paper was published on arXiv on June 24, 2023, with the final version dated December 18, 2023.

I found the paper! Here's what I found about arXiv 2309.17453:

**Title:** "Efficient Streaming Language Models with Attention Sinks"

**Authors:** Guangxuan Xiao, Yuandong Tian, Beidi Chen, Song Han, and Mike Lewis from Massachusetts Institute of Technology, Meta AI, Carnegie Mellon University, and NVIDIA

**Submission:** Submitted on September 29, 2023, with the latest version released on April 7, 2024

**Key Contributions:**

The paper addresses two major challenges in deploying Large Language Models in streaming applications like multi-round dialogue: (1) caching previous tokens' Key and Value states consumes extensive memory, and (2) LLMs cannot generalize to longer texts than their training sequence length. While window attention (caching only recent tokens) is a natural approach, it fails when text length surpasses the cache size.

**Main Finding:** The authors observe an "attention sink" phenomenon where keeping the KV (Key-Value) of initial tokens largely recovers the performance of window attention.

**Framework:** StreamingLLM is an efficient framework that enables LLMs trained with a finite length attention window to generalize to infinite sequence lengths without fine-tuning and can enable models like Llama-2, MPT, Falcon, and Pythia to perform stable and efficient language modeling with up to 4 million tokens and more.

The paper was published as a conference paper at ICLR 2024.

I found the Qwen2 Technical Report for you. Here are the key details:

**Title:** Qwen2 Technical Report

**Authors:** An Yang, Baosong Yang, Binyuan Hui, Bo Zheng, Bowen Yu, Chang Zhou, Chengpeng Li, Chengyuan Li, Dayiheng Liu, Fei Huang, Guanting Dong, Haoran Wei, Huan Lin, Jialong Tang, Jialin Wang, Jian Yang, Jianhong Tu, Jianwei Zhang, Jianxin Ma, Jianxin Yang, Jin Xu, Jingren Zhou, Jinze Bai, Jinzheng He, Junyang Lin, Kai Dang, Keming Lu, Keqin Chen, Kexin Yang, Mei Li, Mingfeng Xue, Na Ni, Pei Zhang, Peng Wang, Ru Peng, Rui Men, Ruize Gao, Runji Lin, Shijie Wang, Shuai Bai, Sinan Tan, Tianhang Zhu, Tianhao Li, Tianyu Liu, Wenbin Ge, Xiaodong Deng, Xiaohuan Zhou, Xingzhang Ren, Xinyu Zhang, Xipin Wei, Xuancheng Ren, Yang Fan, Yang Yao, Yichang Zhang, Yu Wan, Yunfei Chu, Yuqiong Liu, Zeyu Cui, Zhenru Zhang, Zhihao Fan and others

**Key Highlights:**

- The report introduces a comprehensive suite of foundational and instruction-tuned language models, encompassing a parameter range from 0.5 to 72 billion, featuring dense models and a Mixture-of-Experts model.

- Qwen2 surpasses most prior open-weight models, including its predecessor Qwen1.5, and exhibits competitive performance relative to proprietary models across diverse benchmarks on language understanding, generation, multilingual proficiency, coding, mathematics, and reasoning.

- The flagship model, Qwen2-72B, showcases remarkable performance: 84.2 on MMLU, 37.9 on GPQA, 64.6 on HumanEval, 89.5 on GSM8K, and 82.4 on BBH as a base language model.

- Qwen2 demonstrates robust multilingual capabilities, proficient in approximately 30 languages, spanning English, Chinese, Spanish, French, German, Arabic, Russian, Korean, Japanese, Thai, Vietnamese, and more.

- The Qwen2 model weights are openly available on Hugging Face and ModelScope, and the supplementary materials including example code on GitHub.

The paper was submitted on July 15, 2024 and is available at arxiv.org/abs/2407.10671.

Based on the search results, I found information about SpecPrune-VLA by Wang Hanzhen:

SpecPrune-VLA is a paper by Hanzhen Wang and 5 other authors that describes a method for accelerating Vision-Language-Action Models via Action-Aware Self-Speculative Pruning. The paper was published on arXiv on May 24, 2026.

The method is a training-free, two-level pruning approach with heuristic control, and achieves up to 1.57× speedup in LIBERO simulation and 1.70× on real-world tasks, with negligible success rate degradation.

However, regarding your question about ICRA 2026 acceptance and the official GitHub code repository, the search results do not contain specific confirmation that SpecPrune-VLA has been accepted to ICRA 2026 or information about an official GitHub repository for this work. Based on the search results, here's information about the AdaViT paper:

AdaViT was submitted on November 30, 2021 by authors including Lingchen Meng, Hengduo Li, Bor-Chun Chen, Shiyi Lan, Zuxuan Wu, Yu-Gang Jiang, and Ser-Nam Lim.

AdaViT is an adaptive computation framework that learns to derive usage policies on which patches, self-attention heads and transformer blocks to use throughout the backbone on a per-input basis, aiming to improve inference efficiency of vision transformers with a minimal drop of accuracy for image recognition. A light-weight decision network is attached to the backbone to produce decisions on-the-fly and is optimized jointly with a transformer backbone in an end-to-end manner.

The paper addresses a key challenge: While vision transformers achieve excellent performance, they still require relatively intensive computational cost that scales up drastically as the numbers of patches, self-attention heads and transformer blocks increase.

The paper was published at CVPR 2022 and is available on arXiv with the identifier 2111.15668.

Based on the search results, I found information about the paper "Don't Run with Scissors: Pruning Breaks VLA Models but They Can Be Recovered" by Jabbour and colleagues.

The paper addresses Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models and their deployment on resource-limited hardware. The authors observe that pruning VLA models leads to drastic degradation and increased safety violations. They introduce GLUESTICK, a post-pruning recovery method that restores much of the original model's functionality while retaining sparsity benefits.

The paper appears on arXiv (identifier 2510.08464, posted October 9, 2025) and is also available on OpenReview. However, the search results do not contain specific information about acceptance to ICRA 2026, RSS 2026, or CoRL. If you need confirmation about which conference(s) this paper was accepted to, I would need to perform an additional search with more specific conference-related queries.

Based on the search results, I found comprehensive information about the paper arXiv:2112.03227:

## CALVIN: Language-Conditioned Policy Learning Benchmark

CALVIN (Composing Actions from Language and Vision) is an open-source simulated benchmark to learn long-horizon language-conditioned tasks.

**Key Details:**

- **Authors:** Oier Mees, Lukas Hermann, Erick Rosete-Beas, and Wolfram Burgard
- **Publication:** Accepted for publication at IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters (RAL)
- **Submission Date:** Submitted on 6 Dec 2021 (v1), last revised 13 Jul 2022 (this version, v4)

**Purpose and Scope:**

The aim of the CALVIN benchmark is to evaluate the learning of long-horizon language-conditioned continuous control policies, where a single agent must solve complex manipulation tasks by understanding a series of unconstrained language expressions.

**Dataset and Features:**

CALVIN includes approximately 24 hours of teleoperated unstructured play data together with 20K language directives. This is the first public benchmark of instruction following that combines natural language conditioning, multimodal high-dimensional inputs, 7-DOF continuous control, and long-horizon robotic object manipulation.

Code, models and dataset are available at http://calvin.cs.uni-freiburg.de

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's the information about arXiv 2303.04137:

**Title:** Diffusion Policy: Visuomotor Policy Learning via Action Diffusion

**Authors:** Cheng Chi, Zhenjia Xu, Siyuan Feng, Eric Cousineau, Yilun Du, Benjamin Burchfiel, Russ Tedrake, Shuran Song

**Main Contribution:** The paper introduces Diffusion Policy, a new way of generating robot behavior by representing a robot's visuomotor policy as a conditional denoising diffusion process.

**Key Results:** Diffusion Policy was benchmarked across 12 different tasks from 4 different robot manipulation benchmarks and consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art robot learning methods with an average improvement of 46.9%.

**Technical Approach:** Diffusion Policy learns the gradient of the action-distribution score function and iteratively optimizes with respect to this gradient field during inference via a series of stochastic Langevin dynamics steps.

**Key Advantages:** The diffusion formulation yields powerful advantages when used for robot policies, including gracefully handling multimodal action distributions, being suitable for high-dimensional action spaces, and exhibiting impressive training stability.

**Submission Date:** Submitted on 7 Mar 2023 (with the latest version, v5, revised on 14 Mar 2024)

I found the paper! Here's what I discovered:

The paper "Position: Embracing Negative Results in Machine Learning" is authored by Florian Karl, Lukas Malte Kemeter, Gabriel Dax, and Paulina Sierak, and was submitted on June 6, 2024 (not by Bouthillier as mentioned in your query).

The paper was published at ICML 2024 as a main conference paper.

**Main argument:** The position paper argues that predictive performance alone is not a good indicator for the worth of a publication, as using it as such fosters problems like inefficiencies of the machine learning research community and wrong incentives for researchers. The authors call for the publication of "negative" results to help alleviate these problems.

The paper is available on arXiv at https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03980.

I found information about the paper you're searching for. Here are the results:

The paper "Are Transformers Effective for Time Series Forecasting?" was authored by Ailing Zeng and 3 other authors. The full author list includes Ailing Zeng, Muxi Chen, Lei Zhang, and Qiang Xu.

The paper was published in the Proceedings of the AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence, volume 37(9), pages 11121–11128, and was accepted to AAAI 2023 with three strong accepts.

**Main Contribution:**
The paper examines long-term time series forecasting and questions the validity of Transformer-based approaches for this task. While Transformers excel at extracting semantic correlations in sequences, in time series modeling we need to extract temporal relations from ordered continuous points, and the permutation-invariant self-attention mechanism inevitably results in temporal information loss.

The arXiv preprint (2205.13504) has a GitHub repository available at https://github.com/cure-lab/LTSF-Linear with the official implementation of the paper.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about the paper:

Flow Matching for Generative Modeling is a paper by Yaron Lipman, Ricky T. Q. Chen, Heli Ben-Hamu, Maximilian Nickel, and Matt Le from Meta AI and Weizmann Institute of Science that was published at ICLR 2023.

**Key Contributions:**

The paper introduces a new paradigm for generative modeling built on Continuous Normalizing Flows (CNFs) allowing training at unprecedented scale, specifically presenting Flow Matching (FM) as a simulation-free approach for training CNFs based on regressing vector fields of fixed conditional probability paths.

Flow Matching is compatible with a general family of Gaussian probability paths for transforming between noise and data samples, which subsumes existing diffusion paths as specific instances.

A key contribution is Conditional Flow Matching (CFM), where the authors prove that regressing the vector field of conditional paths yields the same gradients as regressing the intractable marginal vector field, bypassing the need to know the marginal score or vector field.

The framework also enables the use of Optimal Transport (OT) displacement interpolation for probability paths, where OT paths are straight lines with constant speed, leading to faster training and easier sampling.

The paper is available on arXiv at: https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.02747

**SparseViT: Revisiting Activation Sparsity for Efficient High-Resolution Vision Transformer**

The paper introduces SparseViT that revisits activation sparsity for recent window-based vision transformers (ViTs). 

**Key Contributions:**
- The method achieves ~50% latency reduction with 60% sparsity through window activation pruning, which becomes possible since window attentions are naturally batched over blocks.
- Different layers should be assigned with different pruning ratios due to their diverse sensitivities and computational costs.
- The work introduces sparsity-aware adaptation and applies evolutionary search to efficiently find the optimal layerwise sparsity configuration.

**Performance Results:**
SparseViT achieves speedups of 1.5x, 1.4x, and 1.3x compared to its dense counterpart in monocular 3D object detection, 2D instance segmentation, and 2D semantic segmentation, respectively, with negligible to no loss of accuracy.

The paper was published at CVPR 2023 and is available on arXiv (2303.17605). You can find the official resources at the arXiv link (https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.17605) and the GitHub repository (https://github.com/mit-han-lab/sparsevit).

I found the paper you're looking for! Here are the key details:

**Publication Details:**
The paper "RoFormer: Enhanced transformer with Rotary Position Embedding" was authored by Jianlin Su, Murtadha H. M. Ahmed, Yu Lu, Shengfeng Pan, Wen Bo, and Yunfeng Liu and published in Neurocomputing 568: 127063 (2024).

**Core Innovation:**
The paper proposes a novel method named Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE) to effectively leverage positional information, where RoPE encodes the absolute position with a rotation matrix and incorporates explicit relative position dependency in the self-attention formulation.

**Key Properties:**
RoPE enables valuable properties including flexibility of sequence length, decaying inter-token dependency with increasing relative distances, and the capability of equipping linear self-attention with relative position encoding.

**Results:**
The researchers evaluated RoFormer on various long text classification benchmark datasets, and their experiments show that it consistently overcomes its alternatives. Additionally, RoFormer is integrated into Huggingface.

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's the information about arXiv 2304.07193:

**Title:** DINOv2: Learning Robust Visual Features without Supervision

**Authors:** Maxime Oquab, Timothée Darcet, Théo Moutakanni, Huy Vo, Marc Szafraniec, Vasil Khalidov, Pierre Fernandez, Daniel Haziza, Francisco Massa, Alaaeldin El-Nouby, Mahmoud Assran, Nicolas Ballas, Wojciech Galuba, Russell Howes, Po-Yao Huang, Shang-Wen Li, Ishan Misra, Michael Rabbat, Vasu Sharma, Gabriel Synnaeve, Hu Xu, Hervé Jegou, Julien Mairal, Patrick Labatut, Armand Joulin, and Piotr Bojanowski

**Overview:** The paper discusses breakthroughs in natural language processing for model pretraining on large quantities of data that have opened the way for similar foundation models in computer vision. These models could greatly simplify the use of images in any system by producing all-purpose visual features that work across image distributions and tasks without finetuning.

**Key Contributions:** The work shows that existing pretraining methods, especially self-supervised methods, can produce such features if trained on enough curated data from diverse sources. The authors revisit existing approaches and combine different techniques to scale pretraining in terms of data and model size, with most technical contributions aiming at accelerating and stabilizing training at scale. Additionally, they propose an automatic pipeline to build a dedicated, diverse, and curated image dataset instead of uncurated data, as typically done in the self-supervised literature.

The paper was submitted on April 14, 2023, with the latest version released on February 2, 2024.

I found the paper you're searching for! Here's what I found:

CogACT is a Foundational Vision-Language-Action Model authored by Qixiu Li, Yaobo Liang, and colleagues. The advancement of large Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models has significantly improved robotic manipulation in terms of language-guided task execution and generalization to unseen scenarios.

**Key Technical Contributions:**

Unlike previous works that directly repurpose VLM for action prediction by simple action quantization, the paper proposes a componentized VLA architecture that has a specialized action module conditioned on VLM output, and systematically studies the design of the action module and demonstrates the strong performance enhancement with diffusion action transformers for action sequence modeling, as well as their favorable scaling behaviors.

**Model Availability:**

The team released three CogACT models with different model sizes, including Small, Base and Large.

The paper was published on arXiv as paper 2411.19650 in November 2024, and the project is available on GitHub and has an official project website at cogact.github.io.

I found the paper you're searching for! Here's what I found:

The paper "Fit and Prune: Fast and Training-free Visual Token Pruning for Multi-modal Large Language Models" by Weihao Ye, Qiong Wu, Wenhao Lin, and Yiyi Zhou was published in the Proceedings of the AAAI Conference on Artificial Intelligence (Volume 39, Issue 21, pages 22128–22136, 2025).

**Key Details:**

The paper introduces FitPrune, a method that generates an efficient token pruning strategy for multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) by removing redundant visual tokens. FitPrune is easy to deploy and designed to meet a predefined computational budget while maintaining model performance.

**Approach:**

FitPrune is a training-free approach that can quickly produce a complete pruning recipe for MLLMs according to a pre-defined budget. It considers token pruning as a statistical problem of MLLM and its objective is to find an optimal pruning scheme that can minimize the divergence of the attention distributions before and after pruning. In practice, FitPrune can be quickly accomplished based on the attention statistics from a small batch of inference data, avoiding the expensive trials of MLLMs.

The arXiv preprint (2409.10197) was first submitted on September 16, 2024, with subsequent updates. You can find the full paper at https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.10197.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

## Paper Information

**Title:** How Far Are We to GPT-4V? Closing the Gap to Commercial Multimodal Models with Open-Source Suites

**Authors:** Zhe Chen and 34 other authors

**arXiv Details:** The paper was published on arXiv as arXiv:2404.16821 in 2024

**Venue/Conference:** It's important to note that the paper with identifier 2404.16821 is an arXiv preprint (published April 25-29, 2024) that presents InternVL 1.5, an open-source multimodal large language model (MLLM). 

However, there is a related InternVL paper by Zhe Chen that **was** accepted to CVPR 2024. The "InternVL: Scaling up Vision Foundation Models and Aligning for Generic Visual-Linguistic Tasks" paper by Chen, Zhe and colleagues was published in the Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (CVPR) in June 2024.

So to clarify: arXiv paper 2404.16821 is the InternVL 1.5 report, while the original InternVL paper (different from 2404.16821) was the one accepted to CVPR 2024.

DINOv2: Learning Robust Visual Features Without Supervision by Oquab et al. was published in Transactions on Machine Learning Research in 2024. It was accepted by TMLR on January 11, 2024.

The paper represents recent breakthroughs in natural language processing for model pretraining on large quantities of data that have opened the way for similar foundation models in computer vision, with these models producing all-purpose visual features that work across image distributions and tasks without finetuning.

I found the research paper you're looking for. Here's what I found:

"Fine-Tuning Vision-Language-Action Models: Optimizing Speed and Success" is a paper by Moo Jin Kim (along with Chelsea Finn and Percy Liang) that studies key VLA adaptation design choices such as different action decoding schemes, action representations, and learning objectives for fine-tuning using OpenVLA as the base model, presenting an Optimized Fine-Tuning (OFT) recipe that integrates parallel decoding, action chunking, a continuous action representation, and L1 regression-based learning objective to improve inference efficiency, policy performance, and flexibility.

The paper addresses the issue that despite recent successes with vision-language-action models, VLAs struggle with novel robot setups and require fine-tuning to achieve good performance, yet how to most effectively fine-tune them is unclear given many possible strategies.

The paper was submitted on February 27, 2025, and you can find:
- The full paper on arXiv at https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.19645
- Implementation code and resources on GitHub at https://github.com/moojink/openvla-oft

Based on the search results, here's the information about the Diffusion Policy paper:

The paper "Diffusion Policy: Visuomotor Policy Learning via Action Diffusion" was published in 2023 in the Proceedings of Robotics: Science and Systems (RSS) held in Daegu, Republic of Korea, on July 10-14, 2023.

The authors are Cheng Chi, Siyuan Feng, Yilun Du, Zhenjia Xu, Eric Cousineau, Benjamin CM Burchfiel, and Shuran Song.

The paper introduces Diffusion Policy, a new way of generating robot behavior by representing a robot's visuomotor policy as a conditional denoising diffusion process.

The paper has the DOI: 10.15607/RSS.2023.XIX.026 and can be found in the official RSS 2023 proceedings, as well as on arXiv and the project website.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about CrossGET:

CrossGET (Cross-Guided Ensemble of Tokens) is a general acceleration framework for vision-language Transformers that adaptively combines tokens in real-time during inference, significantly reducing computational costs while maintaining high performance.

**Key Details:**

- **Authors and Publication**: The paper was authored by Shi Dachuan, Tao Chaofan, Rao Anyi, Yang Zhendong, Yuan Chun, and Wang Jiaqi, with arXiv identifier 2305.17455, and it was published at ICML 2024.

- **Core Innovation**: CrossGET features Cross-Guided Matching and Ensemble, leveraging cross-modal guided token matching and ensemble to effectively utilize cross-modal information, achieving wider applicability across both modality-independent models (e.g., CLIP) and modality-dependent ones.

- **Applications and Results**: Extensive experiments were conducted on various vision-language tasks such as image-text retrieval, visual reasoning, image captioning, and visual question answering, with performance demonstrated on both classic multimodal architectures and emerging multimodal LLMs.

- **Code Availability**: The code is available online, and it can be found at https://github.com/sdc17/CrossGET.


REMINDER: You MUST include the sources above in your response to the user using markdown hyperlinks.

Based on the search results, I found the paper you're looking for:

The paper is titled "Position: Embracing Negative Results in Machine Learning" and was authored by Florian Karl, Lukas Malte Kemeter, Gabriel Dax, and Paulina Sierak.

The paper argues that predictive performance alone is not a good indicator for the worth of a publication. The authors put out a call for the publication of "negative" results, which can help alleviate problems and improve the scientific output of the machine learning research community.

The paper was submitted to arXiv on June 6, 2024, and is available at https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.03980.

Based on the search results, here's the information about the H2O: Heavy-Hitter Oracle paper:

**Conference and Venue:**
The paper was published as part of Advances in Neural Information Processing Systems 36 (NeurIPS 2023) Main Conference Track. It is from the 37th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2023).

**Authors:**
The authors include Zhenyu Zhang, Ying Sheng, Tianyi Zhou, Tianlong Chen, Lianmin Zheng, Ruisi Cai, Zhao Song, Yuandong Tian, Christopher Ré, Clark Barrett, Zhangyang "Atlas" Wang, and Beidi Chen.

**Paper Topic:**
The paper introduces a novel approach for implementing the KV cache which significantly reduces its memory footprint. The approach is based on the observation that a small portion of tokens contributes most of the value when computing attention scores.

**Performance:**
The H2O implementation with 20% heavy hitters improves throughput over three leading inference systems by up to 29×, 29×, and 3× on OPT-6.7B and OPT-30B, and can reduce latency by up to 1.9×.

**Proceedings Access:**
The paper is available through official NeurIPS proceedings and repositories including papers.nips.cc, OpenReview, and arXiv (preprint ID: 2306.14048).

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's information about TokenPacker:

## Overview
The visual projector serves as an essential bridge between the visual encoder and the Large Language Model (LLM) in a Multimodal LLM (MLLM). The visual tokens are redundant and can be considerably increased when dealing with high-resolution images, impairing the efficiency of MLLMs significantly.

## The Problem TokenPacker Solves
Some recent works have introduced resampler or abstractor to reduce the number of resulting visual tokens. Unfortunately, they fail to capture finer details and undermine the visual reasoning capabilities of MLLMs.

## TokenPacker's Approach
TokenPacker adopts a coarse-to-fine scheme to inject the enriched characteristics to generate the condensed visual tokens. Specifically, TokenPacker initially interpolates visual features as a low-resolution point query. Subsequently, high-resolution and multi-level region cues are treated as reference keys and values to inject their finer information to update coarse query via point to region attention in a local context.

## Results
The approach compresses the visual tokens by 75%~89%, while achieves comparable or even better performance across diverse benchmarks with significantly higher efficiency.

The paper was published on arXiv in July 2024 and has source code available on GitHub at https://github.com/CircleRadon/TokenPacker.

## Search Results for RoFormer (arXiv 2104.09864)

I found comprehensive information about the paper. Here's a summary:

**Paper Details:**
The paper "RoFormer: Enhanced Transformer with Rotary Position Embedding" is authored by Jianlin Su and 5 other authors (specifically Jianlin Su, Yu Lu, Shengfeng Pan, Ahmed Murtadha, Bo Wen, and GlenLiu).

**Main Contribution:**
The paper proposes a novel method named Rotary Position Embedding (RoPE) that encodes the absolute position with a rotation matrix and incorporates explicit relative position dependency in self-attention formulation.

**Key Properties:**
RoPE enables valuable properties including flexibility of sequence length, decaying inter-token dependency with increasing relative distances, and the capability of equipping linear self-attention with relative position encoding.

**Performance:**
The enhanced transformer with rotary position embedding (RoFormer) was evaluated on various long text classification benchmark datasets and consistently overcomes its alternatives.

**Implementation:**
RoFormer is already integrated into Huggingface, and rotary positional embeddings perform as well or better than other positional techniques in every architecture that has been tried.

The paper was originally published on April 20, 2021, with the most recent version (v5) updated in November 2023.

I found the paper you're looking for! Here's what I found:

## Paper Summary

The paper addresses the problem that large vision-language models (VLMs) often rely on a substantial number of visual tokens when interacting with large language models (LLMs), which has proven inefficient, and recent efforts have aimed to accelerate VLM inference by pruning visual tokens.

## Key Contribution

The authors find that the cross-attentions between text and visual tokens in LLMs are inaccurate, and pruning tokens based on these inaccurate attentions leads to significant performance degradation, especially at high reduction ratios. They introduce FasterVLM, a simple yet effective training-free visual token pruning method that evaluates the importance of visual tokens more accurately by utilizing attentions between the [CLS] token and image tokens from the visual encoder.

## Publication Details

The paper was published on December 2, 2024, and the authors are Qizhe Zhang, Aosong Cheng, Ming Lu, Zhiyong Zhuo, Minqi Wang, Jiajun Cao, Shaobo Guo, Qi She, and Shanghang Zhang.

The official code is available on GitHub at https://github.com/Theia-4869/FasterVLM, and the paper can be accessed on arXiv at https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01818.

I found the paper you're looking for! Here are the key details:

**Paper Title:** X-VLA: Soft-Prompted Transformer as Scalable Cross-Embodiment Vision-Language-Action Model

**Authors:** Jinliang Zheng, Jianxiong Li, Zhihao Wang, Dongxiu Liu, Xirui Kang, Yuchun Feng, Yinan Zheng, Jiayin Zou, Yilun Chen, Jia Zeng, Ya-Qin Zhang, Jiangmiao Pang, Jingjing Liu, Tai Wang, Xianyuan Zhan

**arXiv ID:** 2510.10274

**Conference:** ICLR 2026

**Overview:**
The paper proposes a novel Soft Prompt approach with minimally added parameters, by infusing prompt learning concepts into cross-embodiment robot learning and introducing separate sets of learnable embeddings for each distinct data source. These embeddings serve as embodiment-specific prompts, which empower VLA models with effective exploitation of varying cross-embodiment features.

**Architecture:** X-VLA is a flow-matching-based VLA architecture that relies exclusively on soft-prompted standard Transformer encoders, enjoying both scalability and simplicity.

**Evaluation:** The 0.9B instantiation (X-VLA-0.9B) was evaluated across 6 simulations as well as 3 real-world robots, with the model achieving state-of-the-art performance across multiple benchmarks.

The official implementation is available on GitHub: https://github.com/2toinf/X-VLA

I found the paper! Here's what the search results reveal about TinyVLA:

## Overview
TinyVLA is a new family of compact vision-language-action models that offers two key advantages over existing VLA models: (1) faster inference speeds, and (2) improved data efficiency, eliminating the need for pre-training stage.

## Problem Being Addressed
Current VLA models face significant challenges: they are slow during inference and require extensive pre-training on large amounts of robotic data, making real-world deployment difficult.

## Technical Approach
The framework incorporates two essential components: (1) initializing the policy backbone with robust, high-speed multimodal models, and (2) integrating a diffusion policy decoder during fine-tuning to enable precise robot actions.

## Key Innovation
The researchers employ a family of small yet powerful vision-language models with fewer than 1 billion parameters.

## Performance
The approach significantly outperforms the state-of-the-art VLA model, OpenVLA, in terms of speed and data efficiency, while delivering comparable or superior performance.

The paper (arXiv 2409.12514) was authored by Junjie Wen and 11 other co-authors, and was published on September 19, 2024.

I found the paper you're looking for. Here's what I found:

**Paper Title:** "Improved Baselines with Visual Instruction Tuning"

**Authors and Affiliation:** Haotian Liu, Chunyuan Li, Yuheng Li, and Yong Jae Lee from University of Wisconsin–Madison and Microsoft Research, Redmond

**Publication:** Published in the Proceedings of the IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition (CVPR) in June 2024, pages 26296-26306

**arXiv ID:** The paper is available as arXiv 2310.03744

**Key Contributions:**

The paper presents the first systematic study to investigate the design choices of LMMs in a controlled setting under the LLaVA framework. The authors show that the fully-connected vision-language connector in LLaVA is surprisingly powerful and data-efficient, and with simple modifications—using CLIP-ViT-L-336px with an MLP projection and adding academic-task-oriented VQA data with response formatting prompts—they establish stronger baselines that achieve state-of-the-art across 11 benchmarks. Their final 13B checkpoint uses merely 1.2M publicly available data and finishes full training in 1 day on a single 8-A100 node.

The improved model is referred to as **LLaVA-1.5**.

I found the paper you're looking for. Here's what I found:

## Paper Details

The paper introduces InternVL 2.5, an advanced multimodal large language model (MLLM) series that builds upon InternVL 2.0, maintaining its core model architecture while introducing significant enhancements in training and testing strategies as well as data quality.

## Key Focus

The work delves into the relationship between model scaling and performance, systematically exploring the performance trends in vision encoders, language models, dataset sizes, and test-time configurations.

## Evaluation Scope

Through extensive evaluations on a wide range of benchmarks, including multi-discipline reasoning, document understanding, multi-image / video understanding, real-world comprehension, multimodal hallucination detection, visual grounding, multilingual capabilities, and pure language processing, InternVL 2.5 exhibits competitive performance, rivaling leading commercial models such as GPT-4o and Claude-3.5-Sonnet.

## Notable Achievement

The model is the first open-source MLLMs to surpass 70% on the MMMU benchmark, achieving a 3.7-point improvement through Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning and showcasing strong potential for test-time scaling.

The paper was published on arXiv on December 6, 2024, with the arXiv ID 2412.05271, authored by Zhe Chen and 41 other authors. You can access it directly on arXiv at https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.05271.

Based on the search results, I found information about arXiv paper 2112.07658 and the naming convention used for this work.

The paper has different titles in different contexts: The initial version (v1) introduced "AdaViT, a method that adaptively adjusts the inference cost of vision transformer (ViT) for images of different complexity", while the later versions introduced it as "A-ViT".

Looking at the official citations, the project page shows two citation formats: one using "A-ViT: Adaptive Tokens for Efficient Vision Transformer" for the IEEE/CVF Conference on Computer Vision and Pattern Recognition publication, and another using "AdaViT: Adaptive Tokens for Efficient Vision Transformer" for the arXiv preprint.

So the answer to your query is that the paper was initially submitted as "AdaViT" in December 2021 (v1), but the title was changed to "A-ViT" in later versions, with both names referring to the same method.

Based on the search results, I found detailed information about the SparseViT paper from CVPR 2023:

The paper "SparseViT: Revisiting Activation Sparsity for Efficient High-Resolution Vision Transformer" was authored by Xuanyao Chen, Zhijian Liu, Haotian Tang, Li Yi, Hang Zhao, and Song Han. The authors are affiliated with Shanghai Qi Zhi Institute, Fudan University, Tsinghua University, and MIT.

The paper introduces SparseViT that revisits activation sparsity for recent window-based vision transformers (ViTs). As window attentions are naturally batched over blocks, actual speedup with window activation pruning becomes possible: approximately 50% latency reduction with 60% sparsity.

The paper was published in the proceedings of CVPR 2023, pages 2061-2070.

The Qwen2 Technical Report was published on arXiv on September 10, 2024 (with the initial submission on July 15, 2024). The report was not published in a conference venue but rather released as a technical report on arXiv, which is the standard practice for many large language model releases.

The report introduces the Qwen2 series with a comprehensive suite of foundational and instruction-tuned language models, encompassing a parameter range from 0.5 to 72 billion, featuring dense models and a Mixture-of-Experts model.

Additionally, the search results show that the Qwen team has since released subsequent technical reports including Qwen2.5 (January 2025), Qwen2.5-1M (January 2025), and Qwen2.5-VL (March 2025), all published as technical reports on arXiv rather than at traditional conference venues.

Links: [{"title":"Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs - ADS","url":"https://ui.adsabs.harvard.edu/abs/2024arXiv241201818Z/abstract"},{"title":"[2412.01818] Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs","url":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.01818"},{"title":"Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs","url":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2412.01818"},{"title":"VisPruner - Qizhe Zhang","url":"https://theia4869.com/VisPruner/"},{"title":"GitHub - Theia-4869/VisPruner: [ICCV 2025] Official code for paper: Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs","url":"https://github.com/Theia-4869/VisPruner"},{"title":"Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs | alphaXiv","url":"https://www.alphaxiv.org/hi/overview/2412.01818v2"},{"title":"[2412.01818] [CLS] Attention is All You Need for Training-Free Visual Token Pruning: Make VLM Inference Faster","url":"http://export.arxiv.org/abs/2412.01818"},{"title":"Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs","url":"https://arxiv.org/html/2412.01818"},{"title":"Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for","url":"https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2025/papers/Zhang_Beyond_Text-Visual_Attention_Exploiting_Visual_Cues_for_Effective_Token_Pruning_ICCV_2025_paper.pdf"}]

Based on the search results, I found information about arXiv paper 2412.01818:

The paper "Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs" addresses the problem that large vision-language models (LVLMs) contain significantly more visual tokens than text tokens, resulting in computational burden, and recent efforts have been made to tackle this by pruning visual tokens early within the language model.

The authors analyze that while most existing works use attention scores between text and visual tokens to assess importance, the text-visual attention score is not an ideal indicator for token pruning.

They propose VisPruner, a plug-and-play method that utilizes visual cues for effective token pruning by first using visual attention to select significant tokens, then removing duplicate tokens based on similarity.

Notably, the paper appears to have had a title change at some point. An earlier version was titled "[CLS] Attention is All You Need for Training-Free Visual Token Pruning", which matches the title reference you mentioned in your query.

Based on my search results, here's what I found about arXiv 2412.05271:

arXiv paper 2412.05271, titled "Expanding Performance Boundaries of Open-Source Multimodal Models with Model, Data, and Test-Time Scaling," was submitted on December 6, 2024, with the lead author being Zhe Chen and 41 other authors.

The paper introduces InternVL 2.5, an advanced multimodal large language model (MLLM) series that builds upon InternVL 2.0, while introducing significant enhancements. The model exhibits competitive performance rivaling leading commercial models such as GPT-4o and Claude-3.5-Sonnet.

InternVL 2.5 is notably the first open-source MLLM to surpass 70% on the MMMU benchmark, achieving a 3.7-point improvement through Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reasoning.

However, I did not find any information in the search results indicating that this paper was accepted to CVPR 2025. The search results show this as an arXiv preprint from December 2024, but do not mention CVPR 2025 acceptance.

I found the paper you're looking for! Here are the search results:

"Flow Matching for Generative Modeling" by Yaron Lipman, Ricky T.Q. Chen, Heli Ben-Hamu, Maximilian Nickel, and Matt Le was presented at ICLR 2023 in Kigali, Rwanda.

The paper introduces a new paradigm for generative modeling built on Continuous Normalizing Flows (CNFs), presenting Flow Matching (FM), a simulation-free approach for training CNFs based on regressing vector fields of fixed conditional probability paths.

Flow Matching is compatible with a general family of Gaussian probability paths for transforming between noise and data samples—which subsumes existing diffusion paths as specific instances.

The paper is available on arxiv at https://arxiv.org/abs/2210.02747, and you can also find the official ICLR poster presentation at the ICLR 2023 virtual conference website.

Based on the search results, I found information about InternVL-related papers accepted to CVPR 2025, though the results show a related project rather than InternVL 2.5 itself:

Mono-InternVL is accepted by CVPR 2025. This is a related model that builds on the InternVL architecture. Mono-InternVL is a monolithic multimodal large language model (MLLM) that integrates visual encoding and textual decoding into a single LLM, with a set of visual experts embedded into the pre-trained LLM via a mixture-of-experts (MoE) mechanism.

Regarding InternVL 2.5 specifically, InternVL 2.5 is an advanced multimodal large language model (MLLM) series with parameter coverage ranging from 1B to 78B, but the search results indicate it was released in December 2024 rather than being accepted as a conference paper for CVPR 2025. The original InternVL model was accepted by CVPR 2024 (Oral).

Based on the search results, here's what I found about arXiv paper 2412.01818:

## Paper Overview

The paper "Beyond Text-Visual Attention: Exploiting Visual Cues for Effective Token Pruning in VLMs" has been accepted to ICCV 2025.

## FasterVLM and VisPruner

The paper presents two approaches for visual token pruning in Vision Language Models:

**FasterVLM:** FasterVLM is a training-free visual token pruning method that evaluates the importance of visual tokens more accurately by utilizing attentions between the [CLS] token and image tokens from the visual encoder. FasterVLM can prune 95% of visual tokens while maintaining 90% of the performance of LLaVA-1.5-7B.

**VisPruner:** VisPruner is a plug-and-play method that utilizes visual cues for more effective token pruning in visual language models. Specifically, it first uses visual attention to select a limited number of significant tokens, then removes duplicate tokens from the remaining ones based on their similarity. By retaining diverse tokens alongside the initially selected important tokens, it maximally preserves the visual information of the input image.

## Performance

Without any training, VisPruner can reduce the FLOPs of LLaVA-1.5-7B by 91% and inference latency by 75%, while maintaining comparable performance. The code is available at https://github.com/Theia-4869/VisPruner.
